# Proposed OEIS comments: A228770 to A231710

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A228791

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A228791>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [9532](../papers/09501-10000/09532-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 5*a(n-1) - 3*a(n-2) for n>3.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Sep 13 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 8 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A228960

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A228960>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [11791](../papers/11501-12000/11791-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: 3*n*(1661*n-1820) *(3*n-1) *(3*n-2) *a(n) +(1927258*n^4 -17091925*n^3 +50171975*n^2 -59448794*n +24442440) *a(n-1) +12*(-705605*n^4 +6363374*n^3 -20228575*n^2 +27219817*n -13185456) *a(n-2) +54*(n-2) *(250934*n^3 -1892927*n^2 +4367836*n -3055963) *a(n-3) -486*(n-2)*(n-3) *(26090*n-34343) *(2*n-7) *a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Sep 15 2013.
> Conjecture 1: the supercongruence a(n*p^r) == a(n*p^(r-1)) (mod p^(2*r)) holds for positive integers n and r and all primes p >= 5. Cf. A350383.
> Conjecture 2: let k be a positive integer, m an integer and let f(x) = g(x)/h(x), where both g(x) and h(x) are finite products of cyclotomic polynomials. Then the same supercongruences hold, except for a finite number of primes p depending on f(x), for the sequence {a_(k,m,f)(n): n >= 0} defined by a_(k,m,f)(n) = [x^(k*n)] f(x)^(m*n). (End)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A229004

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A229004>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10177](../papers/10001-10500/10177-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Wagstaff shows that N(p) = (p^p-1)/(p-1) is the period for all primes p < 102, for p=3 then N(3) = A054767(3) = 13, Bell numbers with indices less than or equal to 13 that are divisible by 3 are those with indices: 4, 8, 9, 11, so the conjecture holds. - _Enrique Pérez Herrero_, Sep 12 2013
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jul 16 2014: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 0. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A229397

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A229397>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [9227](../papers/09001-09500/09227-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 5*a(n-1) - 6*a(n-2) + 3*a(n-3) - a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(4 - 7*x + 3*x^2 - x^3) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 4*x + 2*x^2 - x^3)). - _Colin Barker_, Sep 14 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is carried by a bounded amount of state down the array -- one bit per column where it asks for a parity reaching back to the top, and otherwise a window of three consecutive lines, since every cell it names lies one step away -- so counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 5 states, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Paper: [1002](../papers/01001-01500/01002-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 18*a(n-1) -108*a(n-2) +216*a(n-3) for n>4.
> Empirical: G.f. 12*x^2*(10*x+1)*(10*x-3) / (6*x-1)^3. - _R. J. Mathar_, Sep 29 2013
> Empirical a(n) = 2^n*3^(n-3)*(-45 + 4*n + 16*n^2) for n>1. - _Colin Barker_, Jun 16 2017

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 108 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A230647

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A230647>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [9228](../papers/09001-09500/09228-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 9*a(n-2) -4*a(n-4).
> Empirical: G.f. -3*x^2*(2*x+1)*(x-1) / ( 1-9*x^2+4*x^4 ). - _R. J. Mathar_, Oct 27 2013

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Only the cells with i+j even carry a value, and the diagonal and antidiagonal neighbours of such a cell are again such cells, so the white squares form a board of their own on which the condition looks one line up and one line down. Three consecutive lines therefore settle it for the middle one, and counting a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 5 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A230691

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A230691>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [3659](../papers/03501-04000/03659-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 94 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 152 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 94, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Three consecutive lines therefore settle it for the middle one, and counting a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 27 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 10. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A230802

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A230802>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [11075](../papers/11001-11500/11075-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 68 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 71 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 68, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A230933

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A230933>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [3835](../papers/03501-04000/03835-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 71 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 328 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 71, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A230942

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A230942>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [8921](../papers/08501-09000/08921-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) + 14*a(n-3) + a(n-4) - a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(3 - x + 14*x^2 + x^3 - x^4) / (1 - 3*x - 14*x^3 - x^4 + x^5). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 18 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Only the cells with i+j even carry a value, and the diagonal and antidiagonal neighbours of such a cell are again such cells, so the white squares form a board of their own on which the condition looks one line up and one line down. Three consecutive lines therefore settle it for the middle one, and counting a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 9 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A231129

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A231129>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [3680](../papers/03501-04000/03680-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 90 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 191 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 90, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 182 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 90, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A231399

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A231399>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [3737](../papers/03501-04000/03737-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 83 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1594 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 83, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A231705

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A231705>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [189](../papers/00001-00500/00189-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 77 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 190 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 77, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A231710

Paper: [190](../papers/00001-00500/00190-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrences in the linked file are correct. Column k counts arrays of a fixed width, so it is a walk count in a finite graph and satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one, its order is the order stated for that column, and so is the order of every tail, so any recurrence of that order is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
